### Claude (11-19 19:52)

You're right: it should have been in debug from the start. I've moved it there.

**What changed**
- I cancelled the nine pending tasks of 7302962.
- I kept task 1 running. It is the full `T300_ref` run at the current production settings, now at step 34 with |dE| = 1.5×10⁻⁶ eV, and it will serve as the converged reference for the accuracy check.
- The new debug job is **7314173**, script `R1/fe_r1_speed_dbg.sh`. It runs all five INCAR variants of the 300 K, 128-atom configuration at the same time, as five 64-rank srun steps in one 320-task debug job. That is 5 nodes' worth of cores; debug allows up to 10.
- The variants are:
  - `ref`: the current production settings, as a same-hardware baseline.
  - `fast`: ALGO Fast, EDIFF 1e-5.
  - `vfast`: ALGO VeryFast, EDIFF 1e-5.
  - `fastN`: `fast` plus PREC Normal.
  - `fastR`: `fast` plus LREAL Auto.
- Every run is cut off at 1500 s, 300 s inside the 30-minute limit.
- I dropped the 7500 K configuration so the test fits in one job.
- The job is pending on priority in the debug queue.

**What it measures**
- The main result is the time per SCF step for each setting on the same hardware.
- It also records how quickly the energy converges, step by step.
- Any variant that converges inside 25 minutes also gets a force and pressure comparison against the converged `T300_ref`.
- I extended `fe_r1_speed.py` to report unfinished runs (`--prefix dbg_ --ref T300_ref`).

**Risk:** this is the first time I've launched VASP through srun with PMI2 instead of Intel mpirun. If the ranks fail to start, the job will die in the first minutes and I'll switch to running the variants one after another in short debug jobs.

The 112 recalculation tasks (7297693 and 7297695) stay held. When 7314173 finishes I'll make the table, choose the INCAR and release them. I added this to the debug-QOS memory note, the R1 README and INPROGRESS.

### Claude (11-19 19:53)

OK. I was cautious because of my own note from 14 October, not anything you said. That note records srun with `--mpi=pmi2` failing to start the stock VASP 6.6 binaries: first "MKL not found", then "OFI fi_getinfo() failed". Four test jobs were lost to it, so I switched to Intel mpirun.

Looking at it again, the cause was almost certainly the environment, not srun:
- **MKL error:** that script loaded `load_intel`, which is oneAPI 2026.1, but the binaries need the 2025.3 MKL.
- **OFI error:** no `FI_PROVIDER` was set.

The debug job 7314173 now uses your recipe: `srun --mpi=pmi2`, `I_MPI_PMI_LIBRARY=/usr/lib64/libpmi2.so`, `I_MPI_FABRICS=shm:ofi`, `FI_PROVIDER=mlx`, and the explicit 2025.3 `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`.

If its five runs start cleanly, I'll switch `fe_r1_job.sh` and `fe_a1_job.sh` to srun with PMI2 before releasing the held tasks, so all jobs use your standard launch. I've updated the launch note to say so.

### Claude (11-19 20:48)

The debug speed test is running now as job **7315550**, which started at 20:45.

**Why the first debug job didn't start:** it wasn't queue order. Job 7314173 already had the highest priority among debug jobs. It asked for 320 cores, and the debug QOS allows at most 10 nodes. The free icelake cores were scattered, with only 3 nodes having 30 or more free, so 320 cores couldn't fit on 10 nodes. Slurm estimated a start at 05:44 tomorrow, so I cancelled it. `sbatch --test-only` showed that 128 cores would start immediately and 192 not for about 36 minutes, so I resubmitted with 128 cores and two variants:
- `vfast`: ALGO VeryFast, EDIFF 1e-5
- `fastR`: ALGO Fast, EDIFF 1e-5, LREAL Auto

**srun with PMI2 works:** both runs report "running 64 mpi-ranks … on 10 nodes" and are iterating (`vfast` is in RMM steps, `fastR` in Davidson). I'll switch the production scripts to srun with PMI2.

**Caveat on the timing:** each 64-rank run is spread over 10 nodes, while production uses 2 nodes. Time per SCF step therefore isn't strictly comparable to the converged reference (35 steps × 95 s on 2 × 32). The step count to convergence and the accuracy are comparable. `vfast` against `fastR` is a fair comparison, because both share the same nodes.

The runs stop at 25 minutes. I'll be notified when the job finishes and will then make the table. If the decision needs it, `fast` and `fastN` can go in a second short debug job.

## User (11-19 20:49)

That was way too many cores for a debug job! Write restarts
